Thus, this paper is finished and does not burn in the fire.

...into Rhenish Brandy.

The purified *spirit* is very *subtle* and has neither smell nor taste; or to speak more accurately, it has lost its original smell and taste to such an extent that no one will recognize it for what it once was, nor can anyone say from what it was *distilled*. It can be used for whatever purpose one desires. The labor is not at all expensive, and one can make it in this manner by the whole barrel or in large *quantities*. In the same way, one can also use this *spirit* to great advantage for the preparation of *medicines*, <term>waters of life</term> <note>original: *Aquarum vitæ*; high-proof distilled spirits often used as a base for herbal tonics</note>, and <term>scented spirits</term> <note>original: *Spirituum odoriferorum*; alcohol-based perfumes or aromatic tinctures</note>, thereby saving on more expensive ingredients.

*N.B.* <note>Latin *Nota Bene*: "Note well"</note> If one *distills* many barrels in this manner, subsequently diluting them again with common water—as much as is required for the proof-test of wine-brandy—and then *characterizes* <note>original: *specificiret*; here meaning to flavor or treat the neutral spirit to mimic a specific variety</note> them with the smell, taste, and *color* of French or Rhenish brandy, one can sell whole cellars full of such products and maintain an entire brewery <note>The author uses *Brauerey* here to refer to the entire production facility or distilling business.</note> upon the profits.

# XXXII. A Light that Kindles Itself, etc.

To do this, one first lights a small piece <note>of the prepared twine mentioned on the previous page</note> and experiments with it to see how much burns away in an hour; this length is then multiplied according to the desired time. Next, wrap a small piece of sulfur-thread <note>a "match" or thread dipped in brimstone</note> around the wick of the candle, to which the prepared twine is firmly tied. Then, when the twine burns down to that point, the sulfur ignites, and the candle appears burning at the requested hour. This twine, having been soaked in the aforementioned <term>Solution</term> <note>Referring to the mixture of nitric acid and graphite or asbestos described on the previous page, which acts as a slow-burning fuse</note>, can also be employed for the ignition of mines and for the blasting of ore in the mines at a specific time. In the same way, one can also prepare a pistol or other firearm so that it discharges by itself and fires at a desired hour—namely, by hanging the twine in the <term>Zünd-Pfanne</term> <note>original: *Zünd-Pfanne*; the priming pan of a flintlock or matchlock mechanism</note> or sticking it into the touch-hole.

### 34.
## The Correction of <note>original: *Olei Raparum*; Latin for rapeseed oil</note> Rapeseed Oil, so that it 
->becomes like Almond and Olive Oil.<-

One has a vessel made of thinly hammered lead, in the form of a square basin or trough, about a hand high and as wide and long as one desires. Beneath this, in a warm room protected from the wind, one sets an oven of bricks, so that the trough fits well into it and can have and maintain an equal warmth everywhere. The oven needs no grates, but instead uses a <term>brazier</term> <note>original: *Kohl-Pfanne*; a portable metal pan used to hold hot coals for controlled heating</note>; and thus one has a door made for the oven large enough that one can place a brazier inside. The height of the oven must be adjusted accordingly, so that the fire in the brazier can gradually apply its warmth to the trough or basin. If the trough, and consequently the oven, is large, then one must use perhaps 2 or 3 doors and as many braziers to maintain an equal fire everywhere. Then one pours into the trough clean water about a large finger thick, and upon the water about half a finger thick (no more or less) of common but clean

# XXXIV. Refining of <term>Rapeseed Oil</term> <note>original: *Olei Raparum*</note>.

Apply very gentle fire to the **Rapeseed Oil** in the **charcoal pan**, so that one can hold a finger in it quite comfortably; not warmer, rather cooler, as the oil will otherwise—if the fire is a little too strong—become **yellow** and not **white**. In such a degree of heat, one must maintain it constantly for 4 days and nights without letting it grow cold. In such a time, all impurity, stench, and color will be consumed from the oil, and it will become entirely pure, white, and clear as an **Almond Oil**; and then it is good. And if it should perhaps not become so pure in 4 days—for which air and fire may be the cause—then one must continue with the fire until one finds the aforementioned test with a finger. After this, one must <term>separate</term> <note>original: *separiren*</note> the pure oil from the water and the settled impurity through a tap (which should have been **specifically** attached to the basin beforehand in such a way that one can draw off the oil while the water remains in the basin). In this way it is finished, and one loses perhaps only a 10th part in waste, which mostly draws into the water. 

Now, this oil is suitable on its own to be <term>used</term> <note>original: *adhibiren*</note> in place of common **olive oil**—indeed, even **almond oil**—for all kinds of domestic purposes; and indeed, where one might reject the former due to a <term>suspicion</term> <note>original: *Soupçon*; a French term for a slight trace or suspicion</note> of impurity, this oil, by contrast, is as pure and well-tasting as an almond oil. However, one can transform it into **olive oil** <note>original: *Baumöhl*; literally "tree oil," the common historical term for olive oil</note> if one mixes perhaps a second, third, or fourth part of strong-smelling yet pure olive oil into it; it then becomes so lovely and pleasant that one can sell many barrels as the best olive oil.

->E N D E.<-

A 2

## Kind Reader!

The distribution of the collection of several rare and curious chemical processes—which we obtained following the death of a certain Count from England—has shown that such works were not unwelcome to the devotees of chemical sciences. Furthermore, I have learned from a specific individual, who tested one or another process from that collection and found them to be accurate, that a true treasure of the most secret and beautiful <term>Arcana<note>original Latin: "Arcanis." This term refers to "mysteries" or "secrets," specifically the hidden powers of nature or potent medicinal preparations sought by alchemists.</note></term> is to be found among the remaining processes. I have therefore been moved to share this second part of the collection with the inquisitive reader. We have thereby...

<summary>In this preface, the publisher explains that the success of a previous collection of alchemical recipes from a deceased English Count has prompted the release of this second volume.</summary>
<keywords>alchemical processes, English Count, Arcana, chemical sciences, alchemical collection</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the publisher’s preface from the previous page, finishing the sentence regarding his motivations for publishing these alchemical secrets. Catchword: rium (completing the word "Mercurium").</meta>

## Preface

...no other interest than to serve the public; and should anyone encounter something among these processes which brought him a righteous advantage, so that he could boast of having become a rich man through these **Chemical Processes**, the author asks for nothing more as a reasonable reward for his good intention than that he would have the generosity to report without hesitation to the publisher the **page** <note>original Latin: "Paginam"</note> where something was to be gained. 

In return, one promises him several processes—should such be requested—that are older than 300 to 400 years, and which could be worked without great cost. These are also very close to the true **principles of the art** <note>original Latin/German: "Principiis der Kunst." Here, "the Art" refers specifically to Alchemy, often called the "Hermetic Art" or "Royal Art."</note>, and will be communicated in exchange for a fair compensation. 

This much I can report to the kind reader: that according to these processes, the <term>Universal Work<note>original German: "Universal-Werck." This is the *Magnum Opus* or "Great Work"—the ultimate alchemical goal of creating the Philosopher’s Stone, capable of transmuting metals and healing all diseases.</note></term> can be worked out by means of a single matter—which is mineral and not expensive—using a single furnace and only a single vessel, in not too long a time. The matter seems to be the true **dry water** <note>original German: "trockene Wasser." In alchemical theory, "dry water" (aqua sicca) is a substance that acts as a powerful solvent but does not wet the hands; it is often identified with the "Philosophical Mercury" or a specific metallic salt.</note> of the **philosophers**, by means of which all **metals** are truly unlocked and into their first <term>Mercury<note>original Latin: "Mercurium." This does not refer to common quicksilver, but to "Philosophical Mercury," the primordial, pure essence of matter that alchemists believed remained after a metal was "unlocked" or stripped of its impurities.</note></term>

<summary>The publisher offers to trade ancient, low-cost alchemical recipes with any reader who successfully uses this book to gain wealth, promising a method for the Great Work using a single, inexpensive mineral "dry water."</summary>
<keywords>Universal Work, dry water, Philosophical Mercury, alchemical principles, transmutation, Great Work</keywords>

## Preface

...Mercury <note>original: "Mercurium"</note> can be brought, from which the double **Mercury of the Wise** is afterwards born. This, when it has been sufficiently cooked, can be brought to a state of being a perfect **Tincture** <note>The "Tincture" or "Elixir" is the final, potent form of the Philosopher's Stone used to transmute base metals into gold.</note>. For my part, I have never read anything that agreed more with the writings of the true <term>Adepts<note>original Latin: "Adeptorum." In alchemy, an Adept is someone who has attained the secret of the Great Work.</note></term> than this **manuscript** of which I am now speaking. Therefore, I have held it back for the time being as a **secret**—one that should not be presented to just anyone on a silver platter. Perhaps an opportunity will arise where I might either undertake the work myself—should God grant me life and health and I find more time—or communicate it to a person whom I deem worthy of possessing such an important secret.

I cannot, of course, guarantee anyone that he will unfailingly reach the desired goal with it, because I have not performed the work myself. However, I can certainly assure him of this much: that if he examines this work according to the <term>requirements of the philosophers<note>original Latin: "Desideratis derer Philosophorum." These are the specific criteria or "wants" that historical alchemists looked for to prove a process was authentic.</note></term>, he will obtain complete **satisfaction** through it, and will thus proceed according to the highest degree of probability—and therefore not blindly—

At the very least, one will lose nothing in doing so, for the entire work can be accomplished with just a few talers <note>The thaler was a standard silver coin used throughout Europe and the Holy Roman Empire; the publisher is emphasizing that these experiments do not require the massive investments often demanded by fraudulent alchemists.</note>, which is certainly worth the risk <note>original: "hazardiren"</note>; whereas one often squanders large sums in vain on things that are not grounded in nature at all and which require several years of time. Finally, I wish for nothing more than that, within this collection, such processes <note>In alchemy, a "process" refers to the specific laboratory steps or recipes required to achieve a transformation.</note> may be gathered through which much benefit may accrue to the kind reader, and that he—should he attempt to try any of them—may have spent neither time nor money in vain in his work. etc.

->1.<-
Discovered *Fountain* <note>original: "Fontina," a common alchemical metaphor for a source or solvent.</note> of Count *Bernard of Treviso* <note>A 15th-century Italian nobleman and celebrated alchemist whose works were foundational to later laboratory practices.</note>.

->2.<-
A *particular* <note>An alchemical recipe intended for a specific transmutation or metallic improvement, rather than the "Universal" goal of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> for separating 5 gold ducats from a mark <note>A "mark" (Marck) was a standard unit of weight for precious metals, roughly equivalent to 233 grams.</note> of silver.

->3.<-

->6.<-
*Augmentation of gold* <note>original: "Augmentum Solis." In the symbolic language of alchemy, "Sol" (the Sun) always represents gold.</note>.

->7.<-

->8.<-
To *prepare* *antimony* so that it does not "steal" <note>original: "raubet." In metallurgy, this refers to the loss of precious metals during smelting, where the antimony might absorb or carry away the gold or silver.</note> during the pouring-off process.

->9.<-
An excellent *cement* <note>A "cement" was a mixture of salts, bricks, or other powders used in a furnace to purify metals by drawing out impurities through heat.</note> for low-grade gold or *fixed silver* <note>original: "Lunam fixam." "Luna" (the Moon) represents silver. "Fixed" silver is silver that has been chemically treated to resist heat or acids like gold.</note>.

*Cement* for testing gold, to see if it is genuine or false.

->I.<-

->T. K.<-

# The Discovered Little Fountain of Count Bernhard of Treviso.

Count Bernhard, in his book *On the Chemical Miracle* <note>original: "de Miraculo Chymico"</note>, followed the manner of all alchemical *writers* <note>original: "Scriptorum"</note> by describing the "Great Work"—namely, what the *Matter of the Stone* <note>original: "Materia Lapidis," referring to the substance needed to create the Philosopher's Stone</note> is and how to *prepare* it—in a rather *obscure* and dark fashion. Indeed, it is likely that no one would be able to explain his meaning if he had not, to some extent, clarified himself in an *Epistle to Thomas of Bologna* <note>original: "Epistel ad Tomam de Bononia"</note>. This letter, which he sent quite confidentially as one friend to another, was written in such a way that a *Son of Doctrine* <note>original: "Filius Doctrinæ," a traditional term for a dedicated student or initiate of alchemical secrets</note>, who possesses some guidance and fundamental knowledge in this noble art, might thereby reach the truth and the perfect completion of the work. So that the kind reader may gain a full understanding of the difficulties that usually arise during the *reading* of Bernhard’s writings, I must briefly yet clearly reveal that the entire work depends primarily on two parts, namely: how one [obtains/handles] QUICKSILVER <note>original: "ARGENTUM VIVUM," literally "living silver," the alchemical name for mercury</note>...

<summary>This page introduces a commentary on the famously cryptic works of the alchemist Bernard of Treviso, specifically citing his "Epistle to Thomas of Bologna" as the essential key for students to understand the "Matter of the Stone."</summary>

<keywords>Bernard of Treviso, Thomas of Bologna, Philosopher's Stone, Materia Lapidis, alchemy, quicksilver, mercury, alchemical secrets</keywords>

### I.
## T. K.
# The Discovered Small Fountain of Count Bernard of Treviso. <note>original: "Entdeckte Fontina"; "Fontina" is Latin for a small fountain or spring, often symbolizing the source of the "mercurial water" in alchemical literature.</note>

Count Bernard has, in his book *On the Chemical Miracle* <note>original: "de Miraculo Chymico."</note>, after the manner of all alchemical *writers* <note>original: "Scriptorum."</note>, described the Great Work—namely, what the *matter of the Stone* <note>original: "Materia Lapidis"; the primary substance needed to create the Philosopher's Stone</note> is and how it is to be *prepared* <note>original: "præpariren."</note>—quite obscurely and darkly. Indeed, surely no one would be found capable of explaining him, had he not, in an *Epistle to Thomas of Bologna* <note>original: "Epistel ad Tomam de Bononia." Thomas of Bologna was a physician to King Charles V of France.</note>—which he sent quite confidentially as a friend to a friend—elucidated himself to some extent, and in such a way that a *Son of the Doctrine* <note>original: "Filius Doctrinæ"; a traditional term for an initiate or student of the alchemical arts.</note>, who possesses some instruction and foundation in this noble art, might thereby attain the truth and the perfect completion of the Work. So that the Kind Reader may gain a perfect understanding of those difficulties that usually occur during the *reading* <note>original: "Lecture."</note> of Bernard; I must briefly yet clearly reveal to him that the entire Work depends mainly on two parts, namely: How one [may take] QUICKSILVER <note>original: "ARGENTUM VIVUM"; literally "living silver."</note>

<meta>catchword: VIVUM (completing "Argentum Vivum" or Quicksilver)</meta>

<summary>This page introduces a commentary on the alchemical writings of Bernard of Treviso, specifically highlighting his "Epistle to Thomas of Bologna" as a necessary guide to understanding his otherwise obscure descriptions of the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>
<keywords>Bernard of Treviso, Thomas of Bologna, Philosopher's Stone, Materia Lapidis, Son of the Doctrine, Quicksilver, Alchemy, Chemical Miracle</keywords>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory discussion of Bernard of Treviso's work, specifically focusing on the first of two main parts of the "Great Work": the acquisition of pure quicksilver and its subsequent transformation into an alchemical tincture. It builds on the sentence started on page 13 regarding how to obtain the necessary materials.</meta>

( o )

*pure and clean [LIVING SILVER]*, as he calls his *small fountain* <note>original: "Fontinam"; a common alchemical metaphor for the source of mercurial water.</note>, and secondly, how one should set to work to produce a **tincture** from it.

Regarding the first point, it should be noted that just as **Geber the Arab** <note>Referring to Jabir ibn Hayyan, the legendary 8th-century figure considered the father of alchemy.</note> makes a distinction between the *whole* <note>original: "toto."</note> and between the *parts of the quicksilver* <note>original: "Partibus argenti vivi."</note>, where he teaches that: *The Matter of Perfection is not quicksilver just as it is in its own nature—it is not necessary to have the whole of it—but rather a part of it which has taken its origin from it and was created from it (1) and extracted.* <note>original Latin: "Materiam Perfectionis non esse argentum vivum, quale est in natura sua, necesse ipsum totum sed partem illius, quæ ex illo suam sumsit originem, & ex illo creata est (1) extracta."</note> *Therefore,* he says, *let a selection be made of the purer part or substance of the quicksilver, leaving behind and rejecting the more impure part.* <note>original Latin: "Fiat itaque inquit electio purioris partis, vel substantiæ argenti vivi, relicta & rejecta parte impuriore."</note> In the same way, **Bernard of Treviso** makes a distinction between *raw quicksilver as it comes from the mine* <note>original: "argento vivo crudo, quale venit de minera."</note> and the *pure and clean quicksilver* which is prepared from the *raw* through the process of <term>sublimation|A process where a substance is heated until it turns into vapor and then condenses back into a solid, used here to purify the mercury.</term>. The former is a *quicksilver* of Nature, but the latter is one of Art—though it is prepared and extracted from the natural source.

However, he does not wish for the <term>cleansing|original: "Mundification."</term> of the raw quicksilver to happen through common sublimation with **vitriol** <note>A historical name for sulfate minerals, often used to create strong acids.</note> and **salt**, which is why he also reprimands **Arnoldus** <note>Referring to Arnold of Villanova, a famous 13th-century physician and alchemist.</note>. Instead, it must be done through such things that do not attack the substance of the *quicksilver* or alter and corrupt it. Indeed, he does not reject the superficial cleaning that occurs through distillation, for he says: *I do not truly deny that quicksilver can and should be purified by distilling it twice or four times with common salt, in order to remove its mineral dregs.* <note>original Latin: "Non quidem eo inficias, quin possit ac debeat Argentum vivum, per sal commune bis vel quater distillando purificari, ad se movendam ejus fecem mineralem."</note> But he immediately gives it to be understood that this is not enough, and therefore writes the following
<meta>catchword: Instruction</meta>

<summary>This page explains the first step of the alchemical Great Work: distinguishing between common raw mercury and the "pure" mercury of the philosophers. It cites Geber and Bernard of Treviso to argue that purification must be deep rather than merely superficial.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of preparing the starting material for the Great Work. It quotes Bernard of Treviso and Geber to explain that raw quicksilver must be purified using "perfect bodies" (gold or silver) rather than base metals like lead. It concludes by describing a process of "mortification" and "resuscitation" to produce a crystalline Mercury.</meta>

->✻ ( 0 ) ✻<-

Instructive and ingenious words: *There are certain other sublimations of quicksilver <note>original: "sublimationes, argenti vivi"</note>, which are made from its own bodies, by which it is several times elevated and reunited; at last, it loses and rejects its superfluities, and afterwards it is suitable for the Philosophical Work <note>original: "Philosophico operi"</note> in dissolving metallic species.* <note>original Latin: "Sunt aliæ quædam sublimationes, argenti vivi, quæ fiunt a propriis ejus corporibus, a quibus illud aliquoties elevatum & reconjunctum, tandem suas superfluitates amittit ac rejicit, & postea Philosophico operi convenit, in speciebus Metallicis dissolvendis."</note> **But what those "proper bodies" <note>original: "propria corpora"</note> are, through which the quicksilver <note>original: "argentum vivum"</note> should be sublimated and purified, he teaches shortly thereafter when he says:** *Yet the quicksilver is not altered by this method, but it must have perfect bodies added to it anew, and be altered by them for the Philosophical Work,* <note>original Latin: "Nec tamen hac ratione alteratum est argentum vivum, sed oportet ipsi denuo addi corpora perfecta, & ad opus Philosophicum ab ipsis alterari,"</note> **which Geber <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan, an influential early alchemist.</note> also gives to understand where he writes in chapter 45 of his *Sum of Perfection* <note>original: "Suæ summæ"</note>:** *Quicksilver sublimated with things having affinity with it (unless they are bodies of perfection) is corrupted rather than purified; of this you see an example in lead or the like, from which you sublimate it, you will see it infected with blackness.* <note>original Latin: "Argentum vivum a rebus habentibus affinitatem, cum ipsis sublimatum (nisi sint perfectionis corpora) corrumpitur potius, quam mundatur, at in hoc, exemplum vides in plumbo, vel simili, a quo sublimes illud, conspicies ipsum nigredine infectum."</note> **But how raw quicksilver <note>original: "argentum vivum crudum"</note> should be sublimated and reunited through the "perfect bodies" <note>original: "Corpora perfecta"</note> so that pure and clean quicksilver may be drawn from it, Bernard [of Treviso] certainly remains silent. Whoever is already well-practiced in chemical matters <note>original: "Chymischen Dingen"</note> and otherwise thinks deeply on the subject may easily judge that this must be performed through the <term>mortification|The symbolic 'death' or destruction of a substance's outward form, usually turning it into a powder, to release its inner essence.</term> of quicksilver—namely, that it completely "dies away" alongside the perfect bodies into a red and dead powder, which must perfectly withstand <term>ignition|The application of intense heat to a substance.</term>; then it may be resuscitated by the fire of fusion and yield its <term>Astrum|Literally 'star'; in alchemy, the internal celestial virtue or essential spirit of a substance.</term> in the form of a beautiful, transparent, pure, clear crystalline Mercury <note>original: "Mercurii"</note>, heavy in weight and running again as before.**

It details the alchemical process of mortification, where the mercury is turned into a red powder before being resuscitated into a pure, crystalline state.</summary>

...does not vanish or fly away; for the true **Eagle** <note>In alchemy, the "Eagle" represents the volatile spirit, often associated with mercury, which easily evaporates or "flies" during heating.</note> is very airy and fleeting, flying without wings; so place it in a phial-glass with a long and narrow neck, and seal it at the top; otherwise, it will not remain with you for long. The second part follows, concerning how one should proceed with it toward the **Philosophical Work**. From this, it should be known that **Bernard** <note>Referring to Bernard of Treviso (1406–1490), an influential Italian alchemist whose writings emphasized the use of mercury as the primary material.</note>, in the aforementioned epistle, divided the work into three parts, namely **Azoth**, **Elixir**, and **Tincture** <note>original: "Azoth, Elixir und Tincturam"</note>.

**Azoth** as the first part, he says, **is made from perfect bodies, dissolved by living silver** <note>original Latin: "fit ex corporibus perfectis, dissolutis per argentum vivum." "Perfect bodies" usually refers to gold or silver.</note>.

Therefore, take in the name of the Lord 1 part of **calx of gold** <note>original: "Goldt-Kalck"; gold that has been chemically reduced to a fine, non-metallic powder.</note>, made from gold leaf or prepared otherwise; with this, combine twelve parts of your well-prepared and purified simple **living silver** <note>original: "Argenti vivi"; purified mercury.</note>, through the persistent governance of a modest fire—not too strong—so that a **precipitate** <note>A solid substance that settles out of a liquid.</note> may come of it, but rather that they unite pleasantly, and nothing more rises from the **living silver**. Thus the spirit has congealed with the body, and they flow together at the bottom like supple lead, white at the break like **magnesia** <note>In this context, an alchemical term for a white mineral or the "raw material" of the stone, not necessarily modern magnesium.</note> or **bismuth** <note>original: "Wißmuth"</note>. From this is now drawn the **doubled, two-fold Mercury**, called **Azoth**, provided the mentioned magnesia is held in constant flow in a warm **water bath** <note>original: "Balneo"</note> for approximately 42 days, so that it no longer hardens in the cold but remains running.

**Elixir** is the second part of this high work, and it occurs when the mentioned **Azoth** without ceasing...

Next, take <term>Horn Silver</term> <note>original Latin: "Lunam Cornuam"; silver chloride, which has a waxy or horn-like appearance.</note> and equal parts of the fixed powder; put them into a <term>retort</term> <note>A glass or ceramic vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation and heating substances.</note>, and let them flow together again in a small pot in a sand-bath, placed between bricks. The true Metallic and Mineral universal fiery **Spirit** <note>original Latin: "Spiritus"</note> or the **Soul of the World** drawn in from the air in a magnetic fashion / outside of which no truth can exist in the whole of Philosophy / but through the blessed **Saltpeter** <note>original Latin: "Nitrum"; potassium nitrate, essential in alchemy for its perceived "fiery" properties.</note>, which carries the hidden fire in its belly. All this is put into the **vial** <note>original: "Phiole"</note> / but left open at the top / otherwise the internal fire will shatter the glass into many pieces / and thus it **putrefies** <note>original: "putrificiret"; a process of breakdown or fermentation.</note> in the air / until it begins to ferment / and produces a **froth** <note>original: "Gescht"; the foam or head seen during fermentation.</note> / which rises upward / which indicates / that the **Body** <note>original Latin: "Corpus"</note> has been unlocked. Then take out the **mass** <note>original Latin: "Massam"</note> / put it into a flask <note>original: "Kolben-Glaß"</note> well **sealed** <note>original: "verlutiret"; made airtight using "lute," a specialized alchemical paste or clay.</note> / and draw all the water over [by distillation] / then strengthen the fire / so that a **sublimate** <note>original: "Sublimat"; a solid substance formed when vapors condense back into solid form.</note> follows / grind this into the dregs or **residue** <note>original Latin: "feces"</note>, and pour the distilled water back onto it and draw it off again / thus the water will become sharper; This must be repeated as many times / until the water becomes entirely acidic like a distilled vinegar / but the **sublimate**, which attaches itself to the glass / is always added to it. Pour this **Philosophical Vinegar** / over crushed **Antimony** <note>original Latin: "Antimonii"</note> ore / so that it stands 3 fingers deep over it / then it dissolves the same into a clear transparent redness / when it is fully extracted / then this should again be distilled in a **retort** <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-curving neck used for distilling liquids.</note> with half a part **Salt of Tartar** <note>original Latin: "Sal Tartari"; potassium carbonate.</note> and half a part **purest Saltpeter** <note>original Latin: "Nitri purissimi"</note> / first collecting the watery part / gathered separately / afterward driven with a strong fire / then a **blood-red oil** will pass over with a thousand streaks / bright and clear; Afterward take the red residue at the bottom / from which the oil was distilled / grind it small as well / and pour the **distillate** <note>original: "Phlegma"; in alchemy, the tasteless, watery byproduct of distillation.</note> or

### 5.
### To extract the Mercury from Native Cinnabar and make a Tincture therefrom.

Take: **Native Cinnabar** <note>A bright red mineral consisting of mercury sulfide, the primary ore of mercury.</note> 1 pound / pulverize it well and mix it with 1 pound of **Quicklime** <note>original Latin: "Calcem viv."</note>, half a pound of **steel filings** / and 1 pound of **Martial Regulus of Antimony** <term>Martial Regulus|A metallic mass of antimony purified using iron (Mars); used here to help separate the mercury.</term> / when everything is well mixed together / put it into a retort, to which seal a **receiver** <note>The vessel that collects the distilled substance.</note> / which must be half full of water / and apply heat gradually / and finally from above / so that all the **Mercury** rises over / when everything has come over and nothing more will go / then remove the retort / take out the Mercury / wash it well with salt and vinegar / until it gives off no more blackness / then press it through a leather [cloth] / so it is prepared <note>original: "gerecht"; meaning rectified or ready for use.</note>.

**Note.** The Hungarian native **Cinnabar**, which is mined in the **Spiš** <note>original: "Zipß"; a historical region in present-day Slovakia known for its mineral wealth.</note> not far from the copper-mining town of **Smolník** <note>original: "Schmollnitz"</note> and is **Solar** <note>meaning "of the Sun," implying it contains the essence or "spirit" of gold.</note>, is the best.

## 6.
### The Increase of Gold <note>original Latin: "Augmentum Solis."</note>

Take **Burnt Copper** <note>original Latin: "Æs ustum"; copper that has been oxidized through heating.</note>, heat it until it glows and quench it several times in linseed oil / which will immediately catch fire / therefore you must be ready quickly with a lid and a damp cloth/ and cover it hastily / so it goes out again; just as one is accustomed to do in the preparation of **Oil of Bricks** <note>A traditional distilled oil used in medicine and alchemy.</note>; But it will stink terribly / because of the sulfur it draws to itself; Then take this / once it has become corporeal or metallic, 1 part / as well as 1 part fine silver / and 2 parts fine gold / namely the metal **from the burnt copper** <note>original Latin: "ex ære usto".</note>, let them melt strongly together / afterwards pour it out into a mold / so it still has a bit of "skin" / as all **Crown Gold** <note>High-quality gold alloyed for coinage.</note> usually has. Color or dye this in the following way. Take **Verdigris** 4 **Loth** <term>loth|A traditional German unit of weight, approximately 15 grams or half an ounce.</term> / **Salmiac** <term>salmiac|Sal ammoniac or ammonium chloride, used as a flux to clean metal surfaces.</term> 8 **Loth** and **Nitre** <term>nitre|Saltpeter.</term> 1 **Loth** / rub them well together. But let the augmented gold glow / and then throw it into the said powder / then it begins to smoke and hiss / take it out again immediately/ and throw it into urine / so that it is quenched therein/ such glowing and quenching can be repeated several times / then you will have a beautiful spectacle.

### Burnt Copper <note>original Latin: "Æs ustum"; oxidized copper used as a base for various alchemical transformations.</note> is made in this way.

First melt the copper / which has been made from iron <note>This likely refers to "cement copper," a process where copper is precipitated out of mine water by adding scrap iron—a phenomenon that appeared like transmutation to early observers.</note> (it is brought from Hungary from the **Zips region** <note>Modern-day Spiš, Slovakia, a major historical mining center.</note> and is to be had in **Wrocław** <note>original: "Breßlau"</note> from the dry-goods merchants) make such into sheets / and with the following powder make layer upon layer <note>original Latin: "stratum super stratum"</note> (**Common Sulfur** 1 pound / & **Common Salt** 4 loth <note>A historical unit of weight, approximately 1/32nd of a pound or 15 grams.</note>) half a finger thick / between the sheets in an earthenware pot / which is not glazed / onto the pot seal <note>original: "lutire," referring to "lute," a clay or paste used to hermetically seal alchemical vessels.</note> a lid / which has a small hole / and make a circular fire from a distance around it / as is customary / and afterwards always closer / until finally the sulfur is completely consumed; Know however / that the slower the sulfur burns away / the better it is. Finally break open the pot after it has cooled / and you will find a good **burnt copper**.

->7.<-

### An Extracting-Water to extract all Sulfurs with, without a corrosive.

Take 1 pound of **Honey** and 4 pounds of **Salt** / let it boil in a pot / with constant stirring / until it becomes hard / like a stone / crush the stone small and pour 4 measures of **Wine-Vinegar** upon it and digest it for 2 days and nights / afterwards distill it over the **helm** <term>helm|The cap or "alembic" of a distilling apparatus where vapors collect and condense.</term> / then it is ready. It is a great secret / which serves for faithful instruction.

### 9. An excellent cement for low-quality gold or fixed silver <note>original: "Lunam fixam"; alchemical silver that has been "fixed" to withstand fire.</note>.

### 10. Cement to test gold/ whether it is genuine or false.

### Exaltation of Gold or the Heightening of its Color.

Smelt gold and copper in equal parts <term>ana: A Latin term used in recipes meaning "of each" or "in equal amounts."</term> / and into the best flow throw 1 *loth* <note>A historical German unit of weight, roughly 15 grams.</note> of sulfur <term>Sulphur</term>, which has been sublimated with saffron of Mars <term>croco Martis: Saffron of Mars; an orange-red iron oxide used for coloring and polishing.</term> upon it / when the sulfur has burned off / then pour it out. ### In another way.

Through repeated smelting, draw the redness out of the copper with borax <term>Borrax: A mineral salt used as a flux to help metals melt and to remove impurities.</term> / afterwards smelt the gold with this borax / thus it will become quite high [in color].

### To transform iron into copper / so that no iron remains.

One puts the iron into a melting crucible / and when it glows very strongly in a very fierce fire / and is now about to begin to melt / one throws finely ground sulfur into it little by little / until it is enough. ->15.<-

### A red water which fixes a gold amalgam into the most beautiful gold, such that it yields as much gold as was spent on it, over and above all costs.

Take lime and sulfur <term>Sulphur</term> in equal parts <term>ana: A Latin term used in recipes meaning "of each" or "in equal amounts."</term>, one pound each; sal ammoniac <term>Salmiac: Ammonium chloride, a salt used to clean metals and in the process of sublimation.</term> half a pound; the sal ammoniac must only be added when it is to be put into the retort <term>Retort: A glass or ceramic vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances.</term>. Place this into an open fire in a reverberatory furnace <term>Reverberier-Ofen: A furnace that reflects or "reverberates" heat from the roof onto the material being treated.</term> and distill it step by step <note>original: "gradatim"</note> until nothing more will pass over. Rectify <note>To refine or purify a liquid through repeated distillation.</note> the distilled water 2 or 3 times, then it is ready. Then take 1 *quintlein* <note>A small historical weight, approximately 3.7 grams.</note> of fine gold, make an amalgam <term>Amalgama: A mixture of a metal—in this case gold—and mercury.</term> out of it with 2 *loth* <note>A unit of weight roughly equivalent to 15 grams or half an ounce.</note> of mercury <note>original: "Mercurio"</note>, put this into a retort, and pour this red water over it; digest it <note>To heat a substance slowly and steadily in a warm environment, often for several days.</note> for 8 days and nights, thereafter draw the water off the amalgam several times, thus it becomes mostly fixed <note>Stable under heat; no longer volatile.</note>; apply it to silver, then you will experience what you have.

->16.<-

### A certain and true lead-work to ripen such into silver and gold.

Take 30 pounds of lead that has been calcined <note>Reduced to a powder or ash through intense heat.</note> with sulfur <term>Sulphur</term>, and make a lye from 10 pounds of lime, 7 pounds each <term>ana</term> of potash <term>Pottasche</term>, saltpeter <term>Salpeter</term>, salt, and tartar <term>Weinstein: Potassium bitartrate, a salty crust collected from the insides of wine barrels.</term>; boil it in urine until it becomes thick, then strain it. When the lye is so sharp that it bites feathers in two <note>A traditional test for the caustic strength of an alkaline solution.</note>, then it is right; if not, it must boil longer. Then add the calcined lead to it, let it stand for 2 hours—

### 17.
## A lye with which one can extract the **sulfur** from all minerals and metals.

Mix **saltpeter** <term>Salpeter: Potassium nitrate, a powerful oxidizing agent used in gunpowder and alchemy.</term> and **tartar** <term>Weinstein: Potassium bitartrate, a salty deposit found in wine barrels.</term> together; deflagrate <note>original: "verpuffs"; to burn or explode rapidly with a puffing sound, used here to calcine the materials.</note> and melt them. Once it has cooled, dissolve it in water, and the lye is ready. Now, take a metallic **ore concentrate** <term>Schlieg: Finely ground ore or "slimes" resulting from the washing and preparation of metal-bearing earth.</term> and boil it for several hours in this lye. Afterward, let it cool and pour the clear liquid into a sugar-glass or a cut-off flask and **precipitate** it with **wine vinegar** <term>Wein-Essig</term>. The **sulfur** <term>Sulphur: In alchemy, sulfur represents the principle of combustion and the "soul" or "color" of a substance.</term> will fall to the bottom. **Fix** <term>sigire: From the Latin "figere," meaning to stabilize a volatile substance so it can withstand heat without evaporating.</term> it as you know how; this is a fine secret.

### 18.
## Venetian **stain** for **ores**.

Collect urine from men who mostly drink wine; if such cannot be had, then one takes it without distinction, however one can get it. Fill an oak barrel of one bucket’s capacity or larger. ### 21.
### Separation of silver from antimony by precipitation.

Make a powder of 2 parts **sulfur** / 1 part **salt** and 1 part **calcined tartar** <term>calcinirten Tartaro: Potassium bitartrate (cream of tartar) that has been heated to a white powder.</term> each / cast this upon the **antimony** <term>Antimonium: A brittle, silvery-white metalloid used here as a solvent to purify precious metals.</term> in **flux** <term>Flüsse: The molten state of the metal.</term> / thus it lets the silver fall / which you then purify with **saltpeter** <term>Salpeter: Potassium nitrate.</term>, cast upon it in flux etc.

### 22.
### To separate 3 loth of gold from a mark of silver with little cost.

Take 12 **loth** <term>Loth: A pre-metric unit of weight, roughly 14.6 grams or half an ounce.</term> of fine silver / and 4 loth of gold / melt everything well together / then / when this is done / it is **granulated** <term>gekörnet: The process of pouring molten metal into cold water to create small beads or "grains," increasing the surface area for chemical reactions.</term> in pure water / and afterwards the water is poured off / the granules are dried and placed on a **refining dish** <term>Treibscherben: A flat ceramic vessel used for heating metals at high temperatures.</term> / with a **muffle** <term>Muffel: A clay hood or oven-like cover placed over the dish to protect the metal from direct flame and charcoal while allowing airflow.</term> over the top / and secured on both sides with good clay / so that the muffle stands firm / in the back one makes a nice large hole / so that the smoke can travel out well / when the **martial regulus of antimony** <note>original: "Regulus Antimonii Martialis" — A metallic form of antimony produced by reducing antimony ore with iron (associated with Mars).</note> is with the silver / one adds 8 loth of martial regulus of antimony each time / and lets it smoke away completely / when it is gone and it begins to tremble <note>This "trembling" refers to the "brightening" (Blicken) of the metal, a visual shimmer that occurs when the surface oxide film disappears, indicating the silver is pure.</note> / and looks quite bluish / and pulls itself together / then one must again add fresh martial regulus of antimony, 8 loth each time, only coarsely crushed. This is done 15 to 16 times / then it is enough / and always with a good fire / so that it does not cool / in 2 or 3 hours it has smoked away /

### 26.

Through **mercury**, not the common mercury, but in the manner of the **Philosophers**, then you have the true Art etc. **Bernard** <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490) was an Italian alchemist whose writings emphasized that the "Stone" was made from a single root.</note> says / our work is made from one root / and taken from two **mercurial substances** / drawn entirely raw, pure, and clear / joined together from the mines / and administered through fire / as the **Matter** <note>original: "Materia"</note> requires / constantly cooked / until two become one / and in this one / when they are mixed / the body is made into spirit / and the spirit into body / afterward one must strengthen this fire / until the fixed holds the unfixed body in its color and in its nature / for know / when it is well mixed / then it surpasses everything / and reduces it to itself in its power / and know / that it afterwards **tinctures** <term>Tinctures: In alchemy, the power of a substance to "tinge" or transform a base metal into gold or silver through contact.</term> a thousand times a thousand / and two hundred thousand / this you may fully believe / thus far Bernard.

Through the words "raw, pure minerals," Bernard intended to be understood the **red ores** <note>original: "rothe Erze"; likely referring to the ruby silver ore (pyrargyrite) mentioned on the previous page.</note> / which have never yet come into any fire / and are not defiled with other metallic spirits; these and no others should one take / and the augmentation and strengthening of this fire is to be accomplished with gold and mercury. It is, however, **common gold** / as all **Philosophers** write, but especially **Paracelsus** <note>The famous Swiss physician and alchemist (1493–1541).</note> in his *Book of Minerals, Treatise 1* / composed of the very purest **sulfur** and **mercury** / this same [gold] must be brought back into mercury through our well-known **Red Lion** <term>Rothen Löwen: A symbolic term for a potent alchemical solvent or reagent, often a refined form of antimony or a strong acid capable of "opening" gold.</term> / according to the old and new philosophical way; For our mercury reduces the whole **Body of the Sun** <note>original: "Corpus Solis"; a standard alchemical term for gold.</note>, and makes it volatile / that is, into mercury, and

### The Way of the Ancients Follows.

The long path, which has been tested and approved by many <term>philosophers</term> <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers to alchemical authors and practitioners of the "Hermetic Art."</note>, is this: namely, that the red <term>lion</term> <note>original: "Leo"; a symbolic term often referring to antimony or a specific red oxide of mercury.</note> be taken, which is created most pure by **God** and nature. This should be crushed small and, with warm water, the "mountain" and sand should be cleansed from it as purely as is humanly possible. Then, place half a pound of it into the bottom of a vial glass with a long neck, ensuring that three-quarters of the glass remains empty. This is to be closed with the <term>Seal of Hermes</term> <note>original: "Sigillo Hermetis"; an airtight seal made by melting the glass neck of the vessel shut.</note> and placed in a sand-bath <note>original: "Sand-Capellen"</note>, or in ashes, or in a lamp-fire—whichever of the three one prefers—and governed according to the fourth degree of fire.

First, for 10 days keep it very mild; for the next 10 days stronger; for the next 10 days at a heat where one can still just keep a finger inside the bath; and finally, for 10 days so hot that the sand glows dark red. Meanwhile, the glass will become black through and through, so that one will not easily be able to see through it. Then our <term>mercury</term> <note>The "spirit" or volatile part of the substance being refined.</note> will rise up "alive," and the <term>earth</term> <note>original: "terra"; the solid residue or "feces" left at the bottom of the vessel.</note> that remains at the bottom of the vessel will be quite subtle, like dust, and appear blackish or ash-colored. One must tap the mercury back down and let it rise up again and again until it becomes <term>cinnabar</term> <note>original: "Zinober"; mercury sulfide, prized for its deep red color and alchemical significance.</note> and clings to the walls of the glass. The longer it goes, the more it rises, provided the mercury that is still "alive" is tapped back down; and thus the pure is separated from the impure. It requires, however, a considerable length of time, name-

But my advice is this: that right at the beginning, one should add 1 part of calcined gold to 12 parts of the purified **red lion**; then, as it rises, the matter immediately carries the **soul of the sun** <note>original: "Animam Solis"</note> with it. ## Here follows the short and new process of the blessed Stone of the Wise, according to the opinion of Paracelsus, described in his *Tincture of the Philosophers* <note>original: *Tinctura Physicorum*</note> and in other places.

First, take two parts of well-dried, pure **potter's clay** <term>Potter's clay: A fine-grained natural soil material used here as a base for distillation.</term>, finely sifted or crushed; mix into it one part of good, well-purified **saltpeter**; put them together into a **retort** <term>Retort: A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances.</term>, well sealed with its receiver using **lute** <note>A paste used to make joints airtight.</note>, and distill it with the regiment of the fire, as one is accustomed to distilling such waters; then let it cool, and remove it. Then take crushed **rock salt** <term>original Latin: *Sal gemmæ*; a pure, transparent form of common salt often found in mines.</term> or "Polish salt," as it grows in the mountains; pour the saltpeter-water over it to the height of two fingers' breadth; let it extract well for 24 hours, and it will become blood-red. Pour off this water and distill it once more **by retort** <note>original Latin: *per Retortam*</note>, well secured and dried with an ox-bladder and the **clay of wisdom** <term>original Latin: *Luto Sapientiæ*; a specialized airtight sealing paste used by alchemists to withstand high heat.</term>; then it yields exceedingly strong **spirits** <term>Spirits: Volatile, concentrated vapors or essences extracted through distillation.</term>. Once the spirits have settled, remove the water, and if it is not strong enough, sharpen it once more with rock salt, filter it, and distill it **by itself** <note>original Latin: *per se*</note>, and then keep it well sealed.

## *Solutio* or Dissolution of the White Eagle.

Take the **red lion** <term>Red Lion: A symbolic name for the acidic solvent or the "soul" of gold prepared in previous steps.</term>, which has been washed clean from its mountain; dissolve <note>original: "solvir"</note> it in half a part of the previously **mentioned** <meta>The German catchword "melten" completes the word "gemelten" (mentioned) on the following page.</meta>

### Solution or Dissolving of Common Gold.

Amalgamate one part of gold leaf with three parts of common **mercury** <note>quicksilver</note>, and add to it—weighing as much as the amalgam itself—common shop **cinnabar** <term>Cinnabar: A bright red mineral (mercury sulfide) used here to facilitate the purification or "opening" of the gold.</term>. After twelve hours, let the fire go out, and you will find the gold quite beautifully loose, like a spider’s web. And do not be troubled that you must use and take common gold for this work of yours, for it is not contrary to our "Star," but has sprung and originated from its substance and root; <note>The author translates the following Latin: "For gold is entirely mercury, which is evident from its weight and from its easy combination with mercury itself; therefore, within it lies the total and radical intention and will of the philosophers."</note> take one part of the previously **calcined gold powder** <term>Gold powder: Also called "calx" of gold; the powdery residue left after a metal is heated or chemically dissolved.</term> to three or four parts of the Star of Mercury; dissolve this gold powder in the previously saved water of **nitre** <note>Saltpeter</note> and **rock salt** <note>original: *Nitro und Sale gemmæ*</note>—

## Union of the White Wife with the Red Man.

When you have now individually dissolved and **sublimated** <note>original Latin: *per se eleviret*—to vaporize a solid and have it collect again as a purified solid.</note> 3 or 4 parts of the pure **Star of Mercury** <note>original Latin: *Astrum Mercurii*</note> (or the "Gluten of the White Eagle") and 1 part of the Gold (or the "Blood of the Red Lion"), then pour both **solutions** <note>original Latin: *Solutiones*</note> back together into a glass retort that is well-reinforced <note>Coated with a protective layer of clay or paste to prevent cracking.</note>. Perform a **Marriage or Union of the Sun and Moon** <note>original Latin: *Matrimonium oder Conjugium Solis und Lunæ*—a metaphor for joining the masculine principle of gold (the Sun) with the feminine principle of mercury (the Moon).</note>, the "Union of the Red Man with the White Wife"; which is to say in plain language: throw the seed into the earth. Place another retort in front of it, well-sealed with **lute** <note>A heat-resistant paste used to make airtight seals between glass vessels.</note>, and heat it in a sand bath—well-covered with sand at the top—**by degrees** <note>original Latin: *per gradus*</note>, and finally with a strong fire.

Then the Star of Mercury will rise into the receiving glass, turning somewhat yellow as it comes over, for it carries part of the **Soul of the Sun** <note>original Latin: *Animam Solis*—the essential, coloring principle of gold.</note> with it. One must repeat this work frequently, taking fresh water every third time and saving the old water, until the Gold has risen over, blood-red, with the Star of Mercury. The more the Star of Mercury has taken the Soul of the Sun into itself, and the more often they are sublimated and distilled over together (this may well take 12 or 15 times, always with fresh water every third time)...

### Washing Away the Corrosives.

To wash away the **corrosives** <note>original: *Corrosiven*—harsh acidic substances used for dissolving metals.</note>, proceed as follows: Take the waters that have been distilled over, put them together in a glass flask, and let them settle and rest in gentle heat. Pour a good **Oil of Tartar** <term>Oil of Tartar: A concentrated solution of potassium carbonate, often used in alchemy as a precipitating agent.</term> over it; thus the volatile **Star** <note>original: *Astrum*</note> and gold will settle to the bottom. Pour this off and wash it away carefully with distilled rainwater. 

Then take this precipitated **Star of Mercury** <note>original: *Astrum Mercurii*</note>, as well as that which has **sublimated** <note>Turned from a solid to a gas and back to a solid.</note> in the neck of the retort with the gold or its **soul** <note>original: *Anima*</note>; wash it and distill distilled rainwater from it three times. On the third time, however, so that the **spirits** <note>The volatile essence or vapors.</note> of the corrosive are completely removed, draw off a **Spirit of Wine** <term>Spirit of Wine: Highly concentrated alcohol or ethanol.</term> in a **Bain-marie** <term>Bain-marie: A water bath used to heat materials gently and steadily to the boiling point of water.</term> for the third time—always using fresh spirit—until no more scent of the corrosive water's **spirit** is detected. 

However, when precipitating with the **Oil of Tartar**, one must be very careful that, during the drying process, it is not exposed to great heat or touched with any knife or iron; otherwise, the whole thing will explode like **gunpowder** <note>original: *Büchsen-Pulver*. This is likely a description of "fulminating gold," an extremely shock-sensitive explosive compound.</note>.

### How one should precipitate and make this animated Star of Mercury fixed.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the alchemical process, moving from the washing of the "Star of Mercury" to its stabilization (fixation) and the subsequent increase of its potency through "augmentation."</meta>

<page-num>47</page-num>
<header>✻ ( 0 ) ✻</header>

Once you have well washed your **animated star** <note>original: *animirt astrum*—mercury that has been "vitalized" by the essence of gold.</note>, put it into a vial, seal it hermetically, and let it rise and fall in gentle heat until it becomes stable and red. One should not pay attention to the other colors that appear. However, the exact time in which this **fixation** <term>Fixation: The process of making a volatile substance stable so that it no longer evaporates or changes when exposed to heat.</term> is completed cannot be precisely determined, for as all the **philosophers** <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers to the respected alchemical authorities and authors of the past.</note> report, this depends mostly on the preparation of the material as well as the **governance of the fire** <term>Governance of the fire: The skillful management of the furnace temperature throughout the different stages of the work.</term>. But the proof of its completion is when it remains lying stable at the bottom <note>original: *in fundo*</note> and no longer rises, becoming red like ground cinnabar. Etc.

### Augmentation or Increase.

Once our **medicine** <note>The evolving alchemical substance, often referred to as a medicine for metals and the body.</note> has become stable and red, you may add an equal weight of purified common mercury. Precipitate and fix them together again until the matter remains at the bottom as red as cinnabar and stays stable.

This, however, is only an **augmentation** <note>An increase in the physical quantity of the gold, rather than a true transformative power.</note> for gold and not a **tincture** <term>Tincture: A potent substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold or silver through its concentrated "spiritual" power.</term>, as Paracelsus writes in his book *On the Nature of Things* <note>original: *lib. de Natura rerum*</note>. But if a new **Star of Mercury with the Soul of the Sun** <note>original: *Astrum Mercurii cum Anima Solis*—referring to the mercury combined with the golden essence.</note> were added to this stable precipitate, and these were precipitated and fixed together, then the tincture would increase and grow higher both in **power and quantity** <note>original: *in virtute & quantitate*</note>.

In short, the entire foundation of this art is that one works with nothing other than our gold and mercury; thereafter, the **corrosive agents** <note>The acidic solvents or salts used to break down the metals initially.</note> used for this must be washed away and removed from it again.

Then finally, it must be fixed and once more **coagulated** <note>Turned from a liquid or vaporous state into a solid form.</note> and nourished with its own **mother’s milk** <note>A poetic alchemical term for the essential liquid or spirit that "feeds" the growing stone.</note>; for the more one brings the **Soul of the Sun** <note>original: *Animam Solis*—the spiritual essence of gold.</note> to the **Star of Mercury** <note>original: *Astro Mercurii*</note>, the more the tincture is increased in potency.

### Washing Away the Corrosives.

Regarding the washing away of the **corrosives** <note>The harsh acids or salts used to dissolve the metals.</note>, proceed as follows: Take the waters that have been distilled over, put them together in a flask, let them settle and reside in gentle warmth; pour a good **oil of tartar** <term>Oil of Tartar: A saturated solution of potassium carbonate, used here as a precipitant.</term> over it, and the volatile Star and gold will settle to the bottom. Pour this off and wash it away carefully with distilled rainwater. Then take this precipitated **Star of Mercury** <note>original: *Astrum Mercurii*</note>, as well as that which has **sublimated** <note>To turn from a solid to a vapor and back to a solid, usually collecting in the neck of the vessel.</note> in the neck of the retort with the gold or its **soul** <note>original: *Anima*—the essential life-force or color-giving principle of the metal.</note>; wash it and distill distilled rainwater from it 3 times. For the third time, however—so that the **spirits** <note>The volatile components.</note> of the corrosive are completely removed—draw off a **spirit of wine** <note>original: *Spiritum Vini*—alcohol.</note> in a **water bath** <note>original: *Balneo Mariæ*—literally "Mary’s Bath," a double boiler used for gentle heating.</note> for the third time, always using fresh spirit, until no spirit of the corrosive water can be detected. However, one must take great care during the precipitation with the oil of tartar that it is not placed in great heat during drying, nor touched with any knife or iron; otherwise, it will explode like **gunpowder** <note>This is a practical warning about "fulminating gold," a highly unstable explosive compound that can form during these specific alchemical procedures.</note>.

### How one should *precipitate* and make this animated Star of Mercury fixed.

It uses the metaphor of a "pregnant" red substance to indicate the successful union of mercury and gold essences.</summary>

<page-num>47</page-num>
<header>❊ ( o ) ❊</header>

When you have now well washed your animated **star** <term>Astrum: Latin for "star," referring to the refined, celestial essence of the metal.</term>, then place it in a vial and seal it hermetically; let it rise and fall in gentle warmth <note>This refers to the process of reflux or circulation, where the substance evaporates and condenses back down.</note> until it becomes **fixed** <term>Fix: A substance that has been made stable and no longer evaporates or escapes when exposed to heat.</term> and red. You should pay no heed to the other colors that appear. However, the time in which this **fixation** <note>original: *Fixation*</note> is completed cannot be precisely determined, for it depends mostly on the **preparation** <note>original: *Præparation*</note> of the matter as well as the **regime of the fire** <term>Regiment of the Fire: The art of governing the heat of the furnace through various stages or "degrees."</term>, as all the **philosophers** <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers to earlier alchemical authorities.</note> report. But the test of this is when it remains lying **at the bottom** <note>original: *in fundo*</note> fixed and no longer rises, and becomes red like ground **cinnabar** <term>Cinnabar: Mercury sulfide, a mineral known for its vibrant red color, which alchemists saw as a sign of success.</term>, etc.

### Augmentation or Increase.

When our **medicine** <note>The alchemical substance in its transformative state.</note> has now become fixed and red, you may add an equal weight of purified common **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note>; **precipitate** <note>original: *præcipitiren*</note> and **fix** <note>original: *figiren*</note> them together again until the matter remains fixed **at the bottom** <note>original: *in fundo*</note> and red as cinnabar.

This, however, is only an **augmentation** <note>original: *Augment*</note> of gold and not a **tincture** <term>Tincture: A highly potent substance capable of transforming large quantities of base metal into gold; an "augmentation" merely increases the volume without the same transformative power.</term>, as **Paracelsus** <note>A famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist.</note> writes in his ***Book of the Nature of Things*** <note>original: *lib. de Natura rerum*</note>. But if a new **Star of Mercury with the Soul of Gold** <note>original: *Astrum Mercurii cum Anima Solis*</note> were added again to this fixed precipitate, and these were precipitated and fixed together, then the tincture would rise and increase both **in power and quantity** <note>original: *in virtute & quantitate*</note>.

**In total** <note>original: *In Summa*</note>, the entire foundation of this **art** is that one works with nothing other than our gold and mercury; and thereafter, that the **corrosive agents** <term>Corrosives: Strong acids or salts used to break down the metals at the start of the work.</term> which were used for it are again **rinsed off** <note>original: *abluirt*</note> and washed away.

Then, finally, it must be **fixed** <note>original: *figiret*</note> and again **coagulated** <note>Turned from a liquid to a solid state.</note> and fed with its own **mother’s milk** <term>Mother's Milk: A metaphorical term for the liquid mercurial substance used to "nourish" and increase the growth of the alchemical stone.</term>; for the more one brings the **Soul of Gold** <note>original: *Animam Solis*</note> to the **Star of Mercury** <note>original: *Astro Mercurii*</note>, the more the tincture is increased.

<summary>This page details the fixation process of the alchemical "star" until it turns red and stable, followed by instructions on how to "augment" or increase the substance's volume and potency using mercury and the "Soul of Gold."</summary>
<keywords>fixation, Star of Mercury, Soul of Gold, cinnabar, augmentation, tincture, Paracelsus, regime of fire, mother's milk</keywords>

**Luna** <note>The alchemical name for silver.</note> or silver contains no tincture within itself; rather, the **Philosophers' silver** is the **white body of the sun** <note>original: *Corpus Solis*; this refers to gold that has been stripped of its "soul" or internal "fire."</note>, from which its **soul** <note>original: *Anima*</note> has been extracted with the **Star of Mercury** <term>Astrum Mercurii: The refined, celestial essence of mercury.</term>.

The **Philosophers' gold**, however, is the **soul**, the **sulfur** <note>In alchemy, sulfur represents the principle of combustion and color.</note>, and the color of the gold—which **Paracelsus** <note>A famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist.</note> calls the **rose-colored blood of the Red Lion** <term>Red Lion: A symbolic name for the final red stage of the alchemical process or a potent solvent.</term>.

The **Star of Mercury**, however, is the **glue of the White Eagle** <term>White Eagle: Usually refers to purified sal ammoniac or a specific mercurial sublimate used in the "work."</term>; coagulate or precipitate the two together, and you have the **Tincture of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Tincturam Philosophorum*; the legendary substance used for transmutation.</note>.

->**On Projection.**<-

If you wish to **tinge** <note>To transmute a base metal into a precious one.</note> with this medicine, it must first be cast upon molten gold, and thereafter that same gold must be cast upon molten silver, as is well known to the **artist** <note>The alchemist.</note>.

->27.<-

->**On the Star of the Sun.**<-

<term>℞</term> <note>Recipe/Take:</note> 2 pounds of **gold ore**—the richer the better, yet there should be no native <note>Naturally occurring pure metal.</note> gold among it. Crush this ore very small and sift it through a fine hair-sieve so that it becomes as subtle as flour. Lay such powder about a quarter-inch high upon a glass plate, or else a wooden plate that is coated with **varnish** <note>original: *Fürniß*</note> so that it absorbs neither water nor spirits, and has a rim half an inch high. Place this plate with the ore in the open air from the 23rd of March until the 22nd of June, so that the sun, moon, and stars may shine upon it, the wind may brush over it, and the dew may moisten it; only see to it that no rain falls upon it. Stir the ore well together once every week. After that time, namely after the 23rd day of June, gather the ore together on a warm day when it is quite dry.

Divide it into 2 equal parts; put one part into several vials, seal them shut <note>Literally "melt them shut," a common alchemical technique to create a hermetic seal.</note>, and place them in the **steam bath** <note>original: *Balneum vaporis*; a vessel used to heat materials gently with steam.</note> to **digest** and to **dissolve** of its own accord. When the water has cooled, pour it off, heat the ore to glowing again in the **reverberatory** furnace, and quench it once more in the same dew-water. Repeat this at least 6 times, then **distill** the water until it is dry; thus you will have a pleasant salt. Pour the leached ore onto tablets and place it in the open air; let it stand there for several months, then leach it out again as before; it always yields salt again. However, one must always use fresh dew-water for the leaching. Take the **salts**

Besides this, there is another way which is much easier and shorter to bring native gold—as it comes either from the ores without fire or from gold-washing (otherwise also called "stream-works")—into a running **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note>, living sulfur, and vitriolic salt, in such a way that one receives far more from each piece than in the previous process, namely: There is a **Saturnine** <term>Saturnine: Relating to lead or the planet Saturn; in alchemy, this refers to heavy, dark, or lead-based minerals.</term> black, yet shining, heavy, and greasy **mineral** called *Bleyschweiff* <note>original: *Bleyschweiff*; likely galena (lead ore) or a bismuth-lead mineral</note>, in Latin called *Magnesia* or *Wismuthum Saturninum*, which is the beginning of all metals and minerals. It is unknown to almost no one, indeed not even to the common man, as children often used to play with it. The Indian women are accustomed to dyeing their hair black with it. This mineral—provided it is still living, that is, not yet melted but entirely raw as it comes from the earth—yields a snow-white **sublimate** <term>Sublimate: A solid substance produced by heating a material until it vaporizes and then allowing it to condense back into a solid form.</term> after previous preparation <note>original: *pravia præparatione*</note> during **distillation**, and in considerable quantity. With such "philosophical feathered (leaden) alum" <note>original: *Alumine plumoso (plumboso) philosophico*</note>, one can in a very short time unlock and transform all metals into a running mercury, living **sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur*</note>, and vitriolic salt. This is possible because they are still in their ore and have been, as it were, processed by the stars, air, and **meteors** <note>historical term for atmospheric phenomena</note> during the time of **Aries**, **Taurus**, and **Gemini** <note>The spring months, traditionally considered the best time for alchemical work when "celestial influence" is strongest.</note>. According to what the ore was, so is the **Star** <note>original: *Astrum*; the quintessential essence or concentrated virtue of the metal</note>. ->28.<-

### Process from Mars <note>original: *ex Marte*; in alchemy, Mars is the symbol and name for Iron.</note>

One takes a well-prepared **Saffron of Mars** <term>Saffron of Mars (Crocum Martis): A reddish-orange iron oxide powder used in both medicine and alchemy.</term> that has been prepared with wine vinegar and salt. Reverberate <note>Reverberate: To heat a substance in a furnace where the flames are reflected (reverberated) down onto the material.</note> it with the salt, then leach the salt away and reverberate it once more, so that it becomes quite beautiful. Of this "Saffron," take 1 pound; rub 1 pound of good, pure **sal ammoniac** <term>Sal ammoniac: Ammonium chloride, a salt used to help minerals sublimate (vaporize and solidify).</term> into it and **sublimate** it. At first, heat it gently for 3 to 4 hours, then gradually stronger and stronger until finally the flask glows and the sal ammoniac has risen as a beautiful yellow vapor. After it has cooled, break open the flask; at the bottom <note>original: *in fundo*</note> there will be a beautiful white **residue** <term>Caput mortuum: Latin for "dead head"; the useless or transformed solid remains left in a flask after distillation or sublimation.</term> like silver. It should weigh no more than about 7 or 8 **Loth** <note>Loth: A traditional German unit of weight; 16 Loth equaled roughly half a pound.</note>; if it is more than this, it has not been sublimated correctly.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions following the discussion of the "Junonian Salt." It provides a specific laboratory process for refining a residue (the "dead head") and saltpeter to create a substance capable of transforming silver into gold.</meta>

This **dead head** <term>Dead head: (Latin: *Caput mortuum*) The useless residue or dross remaining in a flask after distillation or sublimation.</term> is taken and **dissolved** in **distilled** rainwater; it is allowed to stand for one night so that the impurities may properly settle. It is then **filtered** and **coagulated** <note>In alchemy, "coagulation" usually refers to the process of thickening a liquid or evaporating it to a solid state.</note> again. One **proceeds** in this manner 6 to 7 times. After this, it is placed in a cellar where it **dissolves** **by itself** <note>original: *per se*</note> into a beautiful yellow **oil**, which is then **coagulated** once more and further **dissolved** until it is ready.

Now one takes saltpeter and **fixes** it with charcoal dust, as is well known <note>To "fix" a substance in alchemy means to make it non-volatile, so it remains solid even when subjected to high heat.</note>. After this, it is **dissolved** in rainwater and **filtered**, then **coagulated** again. Afterward, one pours **Spirit of Nitre** <term>Spirit of Nitre: A historical name for nitric acid.</term> into the **coagulation**; at first, it swallows the **spirit** into itself and finally it **dissolves** the fixed **nitre**. This is dried again, then placed in a crucible and **fixed** once more with charcoal dust. Afterward, let it flow **by itself** in the cellar. Following this, it is dried or **coagulated** again and **dissolved** once more with **Spirit of Nitre**, proceeding as before. When this has been **coagulated** yet again and **fixed** with charcoal dust, the salt is allowed to flow **by itself** in the cellar one more time. Then, take some of this **liquid** and add to it an equal amount of the aforementioned oil. Let them stand together for a couple of days in **digestion** <note>Digestion refers to the process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat, similar to incubation.</note>, and then allow it to dry into a solid.

From this **solid**, one takes 1 **Quentgen** <term>Quentgen: Also known as a Quintlein; a small historical unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 3.75 grams.</term> for every 1 **Loth** <term>Loth: A unit of weight equal to approximately 14.6 grams or half an ounce.</term> of silver in the melt. Let them flow well together for one hour, then let it cool. A reddish slag, like **glass** <note>original: *vitrum*</note>, will then stand on top of the silver. The silver is then separated in **strong water** <term>Strong water: (Latin: *Aqua fortis*) Nitric acid, used by alchemists to dissolve silver and separate it from gold.</term>, and one obtains a beautiful reddish gold-powder. This is added to lead and refined upon the **cupel** <term>Cupel: (German: *Test*) A shallow, porous ceramic dish used in the process of cupellation to separate precious metals like gold and silver from base metals like lead.</term>; the result is a beautiful, high-quality gold, etc.

## A Certain Way to Obtain Mercury from Antimony.

Take 1 pound of **Martial Regulus of Antimony** <term>Martial Regulus of Antimony: A metallic form of antimony produced by reducing antimony sulfide with iron (Mars).</term>, 2 pounds of **Tartar** and **Salt**, and 8 pounds of **Sal Ammoniac** <term>Sal Ammoniac: Ammonium chloride, used in alchemy to help volatalize metals.</term>; all are to be crushed particularly fine, then mixed together and rubbed on a stone for an entire day, because the whole advantage of this process consists in such rubbing; however, during this rubbing, whenever it becomes dry, it must be moistened with **urine** from persons who drink wine <note>Alchemists often specified the diet of the urine donor, believing that the chemical properties of the urine (such as its acidity or salt content) changed based on what the person consumed.</note>. When it has been rubbed in this manner for a whole day, put it into a **glass flask**, pour **urine** over it to a height of 3 fingers, and let it **digest** in a warm place for one month; however, stir the material well every day, and where the urine decreases, pour in fresh urine, repeating this as often as necessary until the month has passed. Then take **crushed glass** and **quicklime**, equal parts of each, add them to this material, and make **small cakes** from it. When these have dried, bury an **iron pot** filled with water in the earth; place an **iron plate** provided with small holes upon it; place your dried **pellets** upon this, and invert another **iron pot** over it. **Lute** <note>To "lute" means to seal the joints of the vessels with a specialized clay or paste to make them airtight.</note> everything well, and build a **circular fire** of coals around it. Give a gentle heat for the first 4 hours, then a stronger fire for another 4 hours; thus a **spirit** <note>In this context, a "spirit" refers to a volatile vapor released by the heat.</note> goes out of the pellets into the water, which changes therein into a **mercury**. Then let everything cool well and stand for a whole night; and this method works well, but it does not yield much.

Others **sublimate** the **Regulus** with **Sal Ammoniac**
